# Legacy exact-string catalogue B: TPO, HR/Recruiter, Super Admin, Public

Generated by static reading of the legacy frontend (no code run). Strings are quoted in JSON form (character for character; `\n` = newline inside the source string, `\"` = a literal double quote), with source line numbers.

## 0. Method and limits (read first)

- The legacy app has NO i18n/locale files (no i18n library in package.json; no useTranslation/intl). All copy is inline JS/JSX in components, so the catalogue is built per SOURCE FILE (each file once) and each page is indexed to its files (section 5 `Page index`).
- Branches: `lms-dev-2026` (63fea105) read via git archive; `prod-2026` = `origin/prod-2026` (e6b09e6d). The LOCAL ref `prod-2026` is stale (see legacy-inventory-B.md section 0) and was not used. Each file header says whether it exists in both branches. Within a both-branch file, line numbers cite lms-dev-2026; strings that exist only in dev are flagged `[lms-dev-2026 only]`; strings that exist only in prod are appended with `[origin/prod-2026 only; line in prod file]`.
- Extraction = regex over source: JSX text nodes (`<Tag>`/`jsx-text`), prop/attribute strings (`title`, `label`, `placeholder`, `okText`, `cancelText`, `message`, `description`, `tooltip`, `emptyText`, `help`, `extra`, `subtitle`, `aria-label`, ...), `message.success/error/warning/info(...)`, `Modal.confirm(...)`, `notification.*`, and any other string literal containing a space or a capitalised word (`string`). Dynamic parts of template literals are kept as `${...}`. KIND column names the syntactic origin (e.g. `<h1>`, `<Button>`, `label`, `placeholder`, `message.error(`), which approximates heading/button/field/toast roles; it does not decide them.
- Known limits: (1) strings assembled at runtime from several pieces appear as separate fragments; (2) strings inside backend responses (server-provided messages such as `res.data.message`) are NOT in the frontend source; (3) enum/status labels held in other utility files are included only if the file is in a page closure or chrome list; (4) the extractor may include a few non-UI literals (false positives) and may miss exotic constructs: treat as the catalogue of record for review, then spot-check the page in the running legacy stack in Round A; (5) table column headers appear as `title` entries of the `columns` arrays.

```js
262:   flat.forEach((item, i) => {
263:     withDividers.push(item);
264:     if (dividerAfterPaths.includes(item.path) && i < flat.length - 1) {
265:       withDividers.push({ divider: true });
266:     }
267:   });
268:   return withDividers;
269: };
```

#### Super Admin grouped tab strips (SUPER_ADMIN_NAV_GROUPS, projects/workspace/template sections, standalone pages) (`src/common/superAdminNavGroups.js`, lms-dev-2026; verbatim, with line numbers)
```js
1: // Superadmin panel's grouped navigation taxonomy — mirrors adminNavGroups.js (college-admin)
2: // exactly for the groups both panels share (they render the identical
3: // src/pages/superadmin/sections/*.js components), plus the platform-wide groups only superadmin
4: // has (Tenant Setup, Feature Controls — see SUPER_ADMIN_STANDALONE below).
5: import { SHARED_GROUP_DEFINITIONS, buildPathToGroup, buildGroupDefaultPath } from "./navGroupDefinitions";
6: 
7: /**
8:  * Projects is defined here rather than shared with college-admin, deliberately.
9:  *
10:  * Superadmin's day in this module is authoring the template catalog and deciding which colleges
11:  * get which template; college-admin's is running their own college's live projects. The two want
12:  * a different first tab and, over time, different tabs entirely — superadmin owns template
13:  * authoring, versions and per-college assignment, none of which college-admin can do. Sharing one
14:  * definition forced both panels to move together and is why the two ended up with the same eight
15:  * destinations regardless of what either role actually needed.
16:  *
17:  * Only things you *monitor* are tabs. Things you *do* — creating, bulk-reassigning, viewing the
18:  * deleted bin — are buttons on the list they belong to, each leading to its own page, so the
19:  * strip stays scannable and nothing here competes for attention.
20:  */
21: export const SUPER_ADMIN_PROJECTS_GROUP = {
22:   title: "Projects",
23:   items: [
24:     { path: "projects/templates", label: "Templates" },
25:     { path: "projects/workspaces", label: "Workspaces" },
26:     { path: "projects/at-risk", label: "At Risk" },
27:     { path: "projects/readiness", label: "Readiness" },
28:     { path: "projects/submissions", label: "Submissions" },
29:     { path: "projects/audit", label: "Audit" },
30:   ],
31: };
32: 
33: /**
34:  * The tab strip shown *inside* one workspace, replacing the list-level strip entirely so there is
35:  * never more than one set of tabs on screen. Each section owns one concern and both shows and
36:  * edits it — there is no separate view page and edit form for the same thing, which was the old
37:  * "which of these two places do I go?" problem.
38:  *
39:  * Archive and delete are not tabs: they live as buttons on Summary, so a destructive action is
40:  * never one stray click away in a nav strip.
41:  */
42: export const SUPER_ADMIN_WORKSPACE_SECTIONS = [
43:   { path: "summary", label: "Summary" },
44:   // The project's narrative content (goal, deliverables, definition of done, personas).
45:   // It lived on Summary only because no tab covered it, which left that page carrying
46:   // metadata and content at once and made its required-deliverables validator block an
47:   // unrelated title edit on any workspace that had none.
48:   { path: "overview", label: "Overview" },
49:   { path: "team", label: "Team" },
50:   { path: "sprints", label: "Sprints" },
51:   { path: "tasks", label: "Tasks" },
52:   { path: "resources", label: "Resources" },
53:   { path: "meetings", label: "Meetings" },
54:   { path: "readiness", label: "Readiness" },
55:   { path: "delivery", label: "Delivery" },
56:   { path: "audit", label: "Audit" },
57: ];
58: 
59: /** Superadmin-only: college-admin can read a template but never author one. */
60: export const SUPER_ADMIN_TEMPLATE_SECTIONS = [
61:   { path: "overview", label: "Overview" },
62:   { path: "sprints", label: "Sprints" },
63:   { path: "tasks", label: "Tasks" },
64:   { path: "resources", label: "Resources" },
65:   // No Meetings tab: template.meetings is stored and re-displayed, but unlike sprints/tasks/
66:   // resources it is never read when a workspace is created from the template — loadProjectMeetings
67:   // has no template-fallback overload, so nothing an admin fills in here would ever reach a
68:   // project. Editing it was a form that did nothing. Real per-workspace meetings still live on the
69:   // workspace's own Meetings tab, where they hit meetingRepository and actually take effect.
70:   { path: "colleges", label: "Colleges" },
71:   { path: "versions", label: "Versions" },
72: ];
73: 
74: /**
75:  * Placements, superadmin's own: the four tabs college-admin also has, plus the two only 42 has, Recruiter accounts (a recruiter
76:  * spans colleges) and Announcements (42 to placement cells). They were lone sidebar rows; all placement work is one place now.
77:  */
78: export const SUPER_ADMIN_PLACEMENTS_GROUP = {
79:   title: SHARED_GROUP_DEFINITIONS.placements.title,
80:   items: [
81:     ...SHARED_GROUP_DEFINITIONS.placements.items,
82:     { path: "recruiters", label: "Recruiter accounts" },
83:     { path: "announcements", label: "Announcements" },
84:   ],
85: };
86: 
87: export const SUPER_ADMIN_NAV_GROUPS = {
88:   academic: { key: "academic", ...SHARED_GROUP_DEFINITIONS.academic },
89:   content: { key: "content", ...SHARED_GROUP_DEFINITIONS.content },
90:   people: { key: "people", ...SHARED_GROUP_DEFINITIONS.people },
91:   placements: { key: "placements", ...SUPER_ADMIN_PLACEMENTS_GROUP },
92:   projects: { key: "projects", ...SUPER_ADMIN_PROJECTS_GROUP },
93: };
94: 
95: export const SUPER_ADMIN_PATH_TO_GROUP = buildPathToGroup(SUPER_ADMIN_NAV_GROUPS);
96: export const superAdminGroupDefaultPath = buildGroupDefaultPath(SUPER_ADMIN_NAV_GROUPS);
97:

107:   { path: "audit", label: "Activity Log", global: true },
108:   { path: "inbound-mail", label: "Inbound mail", global: true },
109: ];
```

```js
51: // Projects are intentionally role-specific: super admins author templates; college admins run workspaces.
52: export function buildPathToGroup(groupsObj) {
53:   return Object.entries(groupsObj).reduce((acc, [groupKey, group]) => {
54:     group.items.forEach((item) => {
55:       if (item.path in acc && acc[item.path] !== groupKey && process.env.NODE_ENV !== "production") {
56:         console.warn(`[nav] "${item.path}" is listed in both "${acc[item.path]}" and "${groupKey}"; the first wins.`);
57:       }
58:       if (!(item.path in acc)) acc[item.path] = groupKey;
59:       const [firstSegment] = item.path.split("/");
60:       if (firstSegment && !(firstSegment in acc)) acc[firstSegment] = groupKey;
61:     });
62:     return acc;
63:   }, {});
64: }
65: 
66: export function buildGroupDefaultPath(groupsObj) {
67:   return (groupKey) => groupsObj[groupKey]?.items?.[0]?.path;
68: }
69: 
...
```
